# Battle Chronicles - knight phase events.
#
# `type = knight` has NO is_ai gate. Unlike `type = commander` it fires for every
# knight on every side of every combat in the world - the probe run saw 41 fires
# from a single battle. Two consequences:
#   - gate hard in is_valid, cheapest test first, and never do expensive work
#     before the gate
#   - bc_chronicle_ready_trigger / bc_mark_chronicle_effect are per-CHARACTER, so
#     a knight's 15-day cooldown is independent of his commander's
#
# Knights push momentum onto the SIDE COMMANDER, not onto themselves. Only the
# commander's advantage modifiers were proven to reach the combat roll; a knight's
# own advantage is unverified, and a knight is the story rather than the mechanic.
# `?=` on side_commander because a side can be leaderless.
#
# TWO RULES LEARNED FROM THE FIRST LIVE RUN, both of which this file now follows:
#
# 1. STATE FIRST, PRESENTATION SECOND. Effects go in the `effect` block; the
#    toast stays purely presentational. Vanilla does the same -
#    00_knight_phase_events.txt applies add_prestige and friends in the effect
#    block and keeps the message presentational.
#
#    The reason ORIGINALLY given for this rule was wrong, and the correction is
#    worth keeping so nobody reinstates it. `send_interface_toast` is documented
#    as "sends a message to the player playing the character in the scope and
#    then executes any effects inside", which reads as though a nested effect
#    cannot run for an AI subject. Measured at this exact site on 2026-08-14 by
#    splitting the count on `is_ai` outside the toast and comparing to a marker
#    inside it: human 0, AI 128, inside 128. The body runs for AI exactly as it
#    does for a human. Keep the rule, drop the justification.
#
# 2. THE TOAST GOES TO WHOEVER IS LEADING THE SIDE. A toast raised on `root`
#    reaches "the player playing root", and nobody is playing a courtier knight,
#    so the player never saw these at all. Vanilla solves it the same way, by
#    re-scoping the message (`liege = { send_interface_message = ... }`). Here the
#    right recipient is the side commander: he is who the knight fought under, and
#    he is who the momentum lands on.
#
# WEIGHTS. The competition for a knight roll is vanilla `knight_none` at
# base = 2000 - twice the commander pool's `commander_none`. Vanilla's own knight
# events sit at base 30-100. AGOT adds only dragonrider events, one of which is
# base = 100000 but is gated behind `is_current_dragonrider_warfare`, so it does
# not crowd anything out for an ordinary knight. base = 150 here is ~7% of a
# valid roll. The previous base = 40 combined with narrow gates produced zero
# fires across an entire session.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A knight holds the breach. The side is losing and one man buys it time.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_holds_the_breach = {
	type = knight

	# Cheapest first: a flag test, then a stat, then the cross-scope strength
	# comparison last. Tooltip rendering evaluates every branch regardless of
	# early-out, but ordering still pays off on the ordinary path.
	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		prowess >= 10
		bc_side_is_losing_trigger = yes
	}

	chance = {
		base = 150
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		modifier = {
			add = 100
			prowess >= 16
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_holds_the_breach"

		add_prestige = 100

		# reverse_add_opinion, not add_opinion. `add_opinion = { target = liege }`
		# changes ROOT's opinion of the liege - it would have made the knight
		# think better of his lord for his own courage. The admiration runs the
		# other way, which is what "Stood Fast in Battle" says, and it is the
		# same direction bc_knight_shames_himself uses for the grudge.
		if = {
			limit = { exists = liege }
			reverse_add_opinion = {
				target = liege
				modifier = bc_valiant_in_battle_opinion
				opinion = 15
			}
		}

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes
			bc_momentum_minor_effect = yes
			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = major KIND = entrenched SWING = up }

			send_interface_toast = {
				type = event_toast_effect_good
				title = bc_knight_holds_the_breach_toast
				desc = bc_knight_holds_the_breach_toast_desc
				left_icon = scope:bc_knight
			}
		}
	}
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A knight shames himself. Non-lethal: he lives, and everyone saw it.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_shames_himself = {
	type = knight

	# `prowess <= 6` never fired once, and it never will: knights are selected
	# for prowess, so a knight that feeble is close to nonexistent. Craven is the
	# better story anyway - the man who breaks is the one who was always going to.
	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		OR = {
			has_trait = craven
			prowess <= 8
		}
		bc_side_is_losing_trigger = yes
	}

	chance = {
		base = 150
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		modifier = {
			add = 100
			has_trait = craven
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_shames_himself"

		add_stress = 15

		# The grudge runs from the liege toward the knight, so it is the liege's
		# opinion of him that drops.
		if = {
			limit = { exists = liege }
			reverse_add_opinion = {
				target = liege
				modifier = bc_craven_in_battle_opinion
				opinion = -20
			}
		}

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes
			bc_faltering_minor_effect = yes
			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = minor KIND = wild SWING = down }

			send_interface_toast = {
				type = event_toast_effect_bad
				title = bc_knight_shames_himself_toast
				desc = bc_knight_shames_himself_toast_desc
				left_icon = scope:bc_knight
			}
		}
	}
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Shieldbrothers. The seed of the wider idea - two knights who came through the
# same press together. Free to hook here because we are already iterating
# knights, and every_side_knight / random_side_knight are proven (probe 14).
#
# This is the only chronicle that leaves something permanent. The opinion decays
# at 0.05/month; the memory lasts forty years, and both men carry it.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_shieldbrother_forged = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		scope:combat_side = {
			any_side_knight = {
				count >= 2
			}
		}
	}

	chance = {
		base = 60
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
	}

	# NOTHING happens before the partner is found. `is_valid` can only ask
	# whether the side has two knights, not whether a second one is off
	# cooldown, so this event can win a roll and still have nobody to pair
	# with. Marking the chronicle up front cost the knight a 15-day lockout for
	# an event that did not happen, and logged a fire that produced no story.
	# A wasted roll is free; a wasted lockout is not.
	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight

		# The one exception to the rule stated just above, and it does not
		# violate it in substance: opening the battle is not a lockout. It
		# writes two variables that every other phase event overwrites anyway,
		# so running it on a roll that finds no partner costs nothing. Do not
		# "fix" this by moving it inside the if - the seam calls below need
		# bc_battle_size, and no other event is guaranteed to have run first.
		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes
		}

		scope:combat_side = {
			random_side_knight = {
				limit = {
					NOT = { this = scope:bc_knight }
					is_alive = yes
					bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
				}
				save_scope_as = bc_shieldbrother
			}
		}

		if = {
			limit = { exists = scope:bc_shieldbrother }

			bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
			debug_log = "BC: fired bc_shieldbrother_forged"

			scope:bc_shieldbrother = {
				bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
			}

			add_opinion = {
				target = scope:bc_shieldbrother
				modifier = bc_shieldbrother_opinion
				opinion = 25
			}
			reverse_add_opinion = {
				target = scope:bc_shieldbrother
				modifier = bc_shieldbrother_opinion
				opinion = 25
			}

			# One memory each, naming the other man. The `shieldbrother` tag has
			# to match the participants list in bc_memories.txt, and it is what
			# the loc reads back as [shieldbrother.GetFullName].
			create_character_memory = {
				type = bc_shieldbrother_memory
				participants = {
					shieldbrother = scope:bc_shieldbrother
				}
			}
			scope:bc_shieldbrother = {
				create_character_memory = {
					type = bc_shieldbrother_memory
					participants = {
						shieldbrother = scope:bc_knight
					}
				}
			}

			scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
				bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = minor KIND = ordered SWING = up }

				send_interface_toast = {
					type = event_toast_effect_good
					title = bc_shieldbrother_forged_toast
					desc = bc_shieldbrother_forged_toast_desc
					left_icon = scope:bc_knight
					right_icon = scope:bc_shieldbrother
				}
			}
		}
	}
}

# ===========================================================================
# BATCH 2026-08-17 - three more knight cases.
#
# WHY. Ten commander templates and three knight ones produced 233 firings in
# 44 battles, so the same sentences repeat inside a single war. The rate
# limiter is the per-character cooldown, not the weights, so a template that
# fires in a slot an old one already owned buys variety at no rate cost.
# docs/specs/2026-08-17-battle-chronicles-cases-design.md carries the argument
# in full.
#
# WHAT IS NEW HERE. Every knight case shipped before this batch gates on
# `bc_side_is_losing_trigger`. A knight on a WINNING side matched nothing but
# bc_shieldbrother_forged, so two of the three below open that half of the
# space, and the third opens ground - an axis no knight event read at all.
#
# WEIGHTS: base = 80, roughly half the shipped 150. The competition is vanilla
# `knight_none` at base = 2000; the three shipped total 360, and three more at
# 150 would have doubled the mod's share of a knight roll. 80 was chosen over
# the commander batch's 30 because a knight roll is a much harder pool to be
# seen in - the file header records that base = 40 with narrow gates produced
# ZERO fires across a whole session.
#
# MOMENTUM STACKING. Each grant below sits behind an explicit
# `NOT = { bc_has_momentum_trigger = yes }` (or the faltering equivalent)
# evaluated INSIDE the side_commander scope. It cannot go in `is_valid`: that
# block is rooted on the KNIGHT, and bc_has_momentum_trigger reads
# has_character_modifier on its own root, so there it would test the wrong
# man's modifiers. The two shipped combat knight events do not carry this
# guard and can therefore put a second live rung on a commander who already
# has one; that is a real defect, but fixing it changes calibrated behaviour
# and is left for a run to confirm rather than folded into a content batch.
# ===========================================================================

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A knight unhorses a lord. The winning-side counterpart to holds_the_breach,
# and the only knight case that grants a major rung.
#
# No death and no maiming: the mod's standing rule. The man goes down, not out.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_unhorses_a_lord = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		prowess >= 12
		bc_side_is_winning_trigger = yes
	}

	chance = {
		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		modifier = {
			add = 60
			prowess >= 16
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_unhorses_a_lord"

		add_prestige = 150

		if = {
			limit = { exists = liege }
			reverse_add_opinion = {
				target = liege
				modifier = bc_valiant_in_battle_opinion
				opinion = 15
			}
		}

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes

			if = {
				limit = { NOT = { bc_has_momentum_trigger = yes } }
				bc_momentum_major_effect = yes
			}

			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = major KIND = bloodied SWING = up }

			send_interface_toast = {
				type = event_toast_effect_good
				title = bc_knight_unhorses_a_lord_toast
				desc = bc_knight_unhorses_a_lord_toast_desc
				left_icon = scope:bc_knight
			}
		}
	}
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A knight takes a captive. Opens the OUTNUMBERING axis, which no knight event
# reads: men yield when the fight is already decided, not when it is even.
#
# The gold is the point of the case - a captive is a ransom - but it is kept
# to 25 deliberately. The mod has no other economic effect and this batch is
# not the place to open one.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_takes_a_captive = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		prowess >= 8
		bc_side_is_winning_trigger = yes
		bc_side_is_outnumbering_trigger = yes
	}

	chance = {
		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		modifier = {
			add = 40
			has_trait = greedy
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_takes_a_captive"

		add_prestige = 50
		add_gold = 25

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes

			if = {
				limit = { NOT = { bc_has_momentum_trigger = yes } }
				bc_momentum_minor_effect = yes
			}

			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = minor KIND = ordered SWING = up }

			send_interface_toast = {
				type = event_toast_effect_good
				title = bc_knight_takes_a_captive_toast
				desc = bc_knight_takes_a_captive_toast_desc
				left_icon = scope:bc_knight
			}
		}
	}
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A knight is ridden down. Ground, which no knight event has read until now.
#
# DISTINCTNESS. `losing` alone would have been a superset of
# bc_knight_holds_the_breach's gate and would only have stolen weight from it.
# The open band narrows it to horse country and gives the case its only reason
# to exist: on open ground the man who breaks has nowhere to break to.
#
# He is not killed and not maimed. bc_wound_effect is not called here - the
# wound family stays the deliberate exception it has always been.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_is_ridden_down = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		bc_side_is_losing_trigger = yes
		bc_field_is_open_trigger = yes
	}

	chance = {
		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			prowess <= 8
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_is_ridden_down"

		add_stress = 10

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes

			if = {
				limit = { NOT = { bc_is_faltering_trigger = yes } }
				bc_faltering_minor_effect = yes
			}

			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = minor KIND = bloodied SWING = down }

			send_interface_toast = {
				type = event_toast_effect_bad
				title = bc_knight_is_ridden_down_toast
				desc = bc_knight_is_ridden_down_toast_desc
				left_icon = scope:bc_knight
			}
		}
	}
}

# ===========================================================================
# BATCH 2026-08-17, THE SECOND - three knight cases about the knight himself.
#
# The six above ask what the knight DID and answer it out of prowess and the
# state of the battle. None of them asks who he is. These three do: one reads
# the ground he is standing on and his skill together, one reads his age, and
# one reads who is commanding the other side.
#
# SAME base = 80 AND THE SAME MOMENTUM GUARD as the batch above, for the same
# two reasons. Do not lift the guard into is_valid; that block is rooted on the
# knight and bc_has_momentum_trigger reads its own root.
#
# bc_knight_meets_his_cousin GRANTS NO RUNG AT ALL and therefore carries no
# guard. That is not an omission - it is the knight-side copy of the choice
# bc_kin_across_the_line made: a man finding his own blood on the other side is
# a thing that happens to him, not a thing that moves a battle. The stress is
# the whole effect.
# ===========================================================================

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# He stands in the snow. The winter band, which no knight case reads - the only
# ground any of them has read until now is bc_knight_is_ridden_down's open.
#
# NO SIDE ROW. Winter is indifferent to who is winning, and the two side rows
# are already spent four ways across the six above; leaving this one open is
# how the case reaches battles the others cannot.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_stands_in_the_snow = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		prowess >= 10
		bc_field_is_winter_trigger = yes
	}

	chance = {
		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		# A man who already holds the trait has done this before.
		modifier = {
			add = 40
			has_trait = winter_soldier
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_stands_in_the_snow"

		add_prestige = 50

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes

			if = {
				limit = { NOT = { bc_has_momentum_trigger = yes } }
				bc_momentum_minor_effect = yes
			}

			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = minor KIND = entrenched SWING = up }

			send_interface_toast = {
				type = event_toast_effect_good
				title = bc_knight_stands_in_the_snow_toast
				desc = bc_knight_stands_in_the_snow_toast_desc
				left_icon = scope:bc_knight
			}
		}
	}
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The old sword. Age, which nothing on the knight side reads.
#
# THE PROWESS ROW IS DELIBERATELY LOW AT 8. The commander-side pair splits age
# into a good half and a bad half (bc_the_old_wolf, bc_the_green_boy); this is
# only the good half, because a knight who is still being sent out at sixty is
# self-selecting in a way a commander is not - commanding is a job you can hold
# from a chair.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_the_old_sword = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		prowess >= 8
		age >= 55
	}

	chance = {
		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		modifier = {
			add = 40
			age >= 65
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_the_old_sword"

		add_prestige = 75

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes

			if = {
				limit = { NOT = { bc_has_momentum_trigger = yes } }
				bc_momentum_minor_effect = yes
			}

			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = minor KIND = ordered SWING = up }

			send_interface_toast = {
				type = event_toast_effect_good
				title = bc_knight_the_old_sword_toast
				desc = bc_knight_the_old_sword_toast_desc
				left_icon = scope:bc_knight
			}
		}
	}
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# He meets his cousin. bc_foe_is_kin_trigger, asked about the KNIGHT.
#
# THE TRIGGER IS ROOT-SAFE AND THAT IS WHY IT CAN BE USED HERE UNCHANGED. It
# calls `is_close_family_of` on its own root with the enemy commander as the
# target, and it walks to that commander through scope:combat_side, which is
# set for a knight event exactly as it is for a commander one. So rooted on a
# knight it asks whether THE KNIGHT is kin to the man opposite, which is a
# different question from the one bc_kin_across_the_line asks and can be true
# on a battle where that one is false.
#
# NO RUNG, NO GUARD, NO SIDE ROW. Only the stress and the record.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_meets_his_cousin = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		bc_foe_is_kin_trigger = yes
	}

	chance = {
		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		# It weighs more on a man built to feel it.
		modifier = {
			add = 40
			has_trait = compassionate
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_trait = callous
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_meets_his_cousin"

		add_stress = 15

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes
			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = minor KIND = measured SWING = down }

			send_interface_toast = {
				type = event_toast_effect_neutral
				title = bc_knight_meets_his_cousin_toast
				desc = bc_knight_meets_his_cousin_toast_desc
				left_icon = scope:bc_knight
			}
		}
	}
}

# ===========================================================================
# BATCH 2026-08-17, THE THIRD - three knight cases about the battle around him.
#
# The nine above read the knight (prowess, age, kin, craven) and the side he is
# on (winning, losing, outnumbering). None of them reads the BATTLE ITSELF -
# how bloody it has got, how long the war behind it has run, how many men are
# on the field. All three of those triggers were written for commander cases
# and have never been asked from a knight's root. They are all rooted on a
# character but read only scope:combat_side, which a knight event has, so they
# work here unchanged - the same argument bc_knight_meets_his_cousin makes
# about bc_foe_is_kin_trigger.
#
# SAME base = 80, same momentum guard inside the side_commander block, for the
# same reasons as the two batches above.
#
# NONE OF THE THREE IS A SUBSET of a shipped gate. bc_knight_in_the_press
# shares prowess >= 12 with bc_knight_unhorses_a_lord but not its winning row,
# and a losing side can have killed two fifths of the enemy. bc_knight_the_long
# _war shares prowess <= 8 with bc_knight_shames_himself's OR but not its losing
# row. bc_knight_among_thousands has no row any other knight case has.
# ===========================================================================

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# In the press. bc_field_is_bloody_trigger, which two commander cases read and
# no knight case ever has.
#
# THE COMMANDER SEES A TALLY, THE KNIGHT SEES THE GROUND. Same trigger, and
# that is the point: bc_the_killing_is_general says two fifths of them are down
# and the line is going forward, this one says a man cannot put his feet
# anywhere clean. Both can be true of the same five days.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_in_the_press = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		prowess >= 12
		bc_field_is_bloody_trigger = yes
	}

	chance = {
		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		# A man who does not mind the work does more of it.
		modifier = {
			add = 40
			has_trait = sadistic
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_trait = compassionate
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_in_the_press"

		add_prestige = 50

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes

			if = {
				limit = { NOT = { bc_has_momentum_trigger = yes } }
				bc_momentum_minor_effect = yes
			}

			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = minor KIND = bloodied SWING = up }

			send_interface_toast = {
				type = event_toast_effect_good
				title = bc_knight_in_the_press_toast
				desc = bc_knight_in_the_press_toast_desc
				left_icon = scope:bc_knight
			}
		}
	}
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Two years of this. bc_war_is_long_trigger, asked about a man who was never
# much good at it.
#
# THE PROWESS ROW IS THE CASE, not decoration. The long war on its own is
# bc_the_long_war's ground on the commander side; what makes this its own story
# is that it happens to the worst man in the line rather than to the army. A
# good knight two years into a war is bc_knight_the_old_sword's territory.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_the_long_war = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes

		bc_war_is_long_trigger = yes

		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		modifier = {
			add = 40
			has_trait = craven
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_trait = brave
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Among thousands. Fifteen thousand men on the field, read from inside the
# ranks - the knight-side answer to bc_too_wide_to_see.
#
# NO SECOND ROW AND NO RUNG. The commander's version of this grants faltering
# because a host he cannot see is a host he cannot steer; a knight in the same
# host is not steering anything, so nothing about the battle changes and the
# record is all that is written. That also makes this the cheapest knight case
# in the file, which is what a gate with a single row should be.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_among_thousands = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes

		bc_host_is_vast_trigger = yes

		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		# A man who wanted to be seen minds it more.
		modifier = {
			add = 40
			has_trait = ambitious
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_among_thousands"

		}
	}
}

# ===========================================================================
# BATCH 2026-08-17, THE FIFTH ON THIS SIDE: WHAT KIND OF MAN HE IS
#
# THE KNIGHT SIDE HAS NEVER GATED ON A TRAIT. Twelve shipped knight cases and
# every one of them is gated on prowess, on age, on the ground, on the battle or
# on the man opposite; the only `has_trait` in a knight `is_valid` at all is the
# `craven` half of bc_knight_shames_himself's OR, where it is an alternative to a
# prowess floor rather than a case of its own. Traits appear ELEVEN times in the
# file as `chance` rows and never once as a reason. The commander side got its
# character batch weeks ago - this is the knight-side copy of it.
#
# PROWESS SAYS WHAT HE CAN DO AND A TRAIT SAYS WHAT HE WILL DO, which is why
# none of the three below carries a prowess row. A prowess floor would say "and
# he is also good", turning a story about temperament into a second story about
# skill, and the file already has six of those.
#
# NONE OF THE THREE IS A SUBSET of a shipped gate:
#   - the glory hound has no second row at all, and no other knight case is
#     gated on a trait alone
#   - walking them back shares `bc_side_is_losing_trigger` with three cases and
#     none of their other rows: holds_the_breach wants prowess >= 10, shames
#     himself wants craven or prowess <= 8, is_ridden_down wants open ground
#   - stripping the dead shares `bc_side_is_winning_trigger` with two, and both
#     of those want prowess (>= 12, and >= 8 with outnumbering)
#
# ONE OF THEM FILLS THE LAST EMPTY CELL IN THE SEAM. bc_knight_walks_them_back
# is the mod's first `ordered`/`down`, the one (KIND, SWING) pair that no call
# site had ever used - see the re-counted census in bc_ledger_effects.txt. The
# verb ladder deliberately maps it to nothing, so this template hands out no
# army modifier and no rung; it writes a record and pays a little prestige. That
# is the whole design of the pair: a line that keeps its shape while it loses is
# worth remembering and is not worth a bonus.
# ===========================================================================

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The glory hound. Gated on `arrogant` and nothing else, the first knight case
# in the file whose only row is a trait.
#
# HE IS PAID AND THE ARMY IS CHARGED. The prestige is his, the momentum is real,
# and `wild` hands the side bc_spent_horse for it - a man who goes early and
# alone is worth watching and costs the line its horses. Two commander cases do
# the same trade (bc_the_wrath_takes_him, bc_the_old_grudge) and this is the
# knight-side third.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_the_glory_hound = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		has_trait = arrogant
	}

	chance = {
		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		# Wanting to be seen and wanting to rise are different appetites that
		# feed each other.
		modifier = {
			add = 40
			has_trait = ambitious
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_trait = humble
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_the_glory_hound"

		add_prestige = 75

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes

			if = {
				limit = { NOT = { bc_has_momentum_trigger = yes } }
				bc_momentum_minor_effect = yes
			}

			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = minor KIND = wild SWING = up }

			send_interface_toast = {
				type = event_toast_effect_good
				title = bc_knight_the_glory_hound_toast
				desc = bc_knight_the_glory_hound_toast_desc
				left_icon = scope:bc_knight
			}
		}
	}
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# He walks them back. A patient man on a side that is losing, keeping the
# withdrawal from becoming a rout.
#
# THE MOD'S FIRST `ordered`/`down`. It grants no rung in either direction, and
# that is the case rather than an omission: momentum would say the retreat was
# secretly a victory, and faltering would say a man who held the line together
# made things worse. The prestige is small because nobody sings about this.
#
# THIS IS NOT bc_knight_shames_himself INVERTED. That one is a bad man on a
# losing side and the side loses harder for it; this one is a steady man on a
# losing side and the side loses no worse. Both can fire in the same battle to
# two different knights, and a player reading them together is reading the
# argument the pair exists to make.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_walks_them_back = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		has_trait = patient
		bc_side_is_losing_trigger = yes
	}

	chance = {
		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		# A man who thinks the rest of them are owed something does this more
		# readily; a man in a hurry does it less.
		modifier = {
			add = 40
			has_trait = just
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_trait = impatient
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_walks_them_back"

		add_prestige = 25

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes
			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = minor KIND = ordered SWING = down }

			send_interface_toast = {
				type = event_toast_effect_neutral
				title = bc_knight_walks_them_back_toast
				desc = bc_knight_walks_them_back_toast_desc
				left_icon = scope:bc_knight
			}
		}
	}
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# He stops to strip the dead. A greedy man on a side that is already winning,
# who decides the winning can proceed without him.
#
# THE ONLY KNIGHT CASE THAT PAYS IN GOLD AND COSTS THE SIDE SOMETHING, which is
# the point: he is better off and the line is thinner. bc_knight_takes_a_captive
# is the honest version of the same appetite - it pays gold too, but it is gated
# on prowess and on outnumbering, and the man is still in the fight.
#
# THE WINNING ROW IS LOAD-BEARING. A man who wanders off to loot while his side
# is losing is bc_knight_shames_himself's story and is already told; what is new
# here is that the side can afford him, and pays anyway.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_strips_the_dead = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		has_trait = greedy
		bc_side_is_winning_trigger = yes
	}

	chance = {
		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		# Appetite and idleness compound; a man who gives it away has less
		# reason to stoop for it.
		modifier = {
			add = 40
			has_trait = lazy
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_trait = generous
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_strips_the_dead"

		add_gold = 25

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes

			if = {
				limit = { NOT = { bc_is_faltering_trigger = yes } }
				bc_faltering_minor_effect = yes
			}

			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = minor KIND = wild SWING = down }

			send_interface_toast = {
				type = event_toast_effect_bad
				title = bc_knight_strips_the_dead_toast
				desc = bc_knight_strips_the_dead_toast_desc
				left_icon = scope:bc_knight
			}
		}
	}
}

# ===========================================================================
# BATCH 2026-08-17, THE SIXTH ON THIS SIDE: THE GROUND, FROM INSIDE THE RANKS
#
# THE KNIGHT SIDE READS TWO OF THE SIX BANDS. bc_knight_is_ridden_down wants
# open and bc_knight_stands_in_the_snow wants winter, and that is the whole of
# it, while the commander side now gates on all six. These three add the two
# commonest bands in the game as played and the rarest that is not dead.
#
# THE CENSUS DECIDES WHICH BANDS ARE WORTH A TEMPLATE AND HOW TIGHTLY EACH IS
# GATED. The DIAG run's Q5 terrain sample, 137 battles: rough 58, forest 32,
# winter 20, open 19, jungle 8, desert 0. So rough carries a second row because
# a gate on it alone would fire constantly; jungle carries none because eight in
# a hundred and thirty-seven is already rare; and DESERT GETS NO KNIGHT CASE AT
# ALL, because it scored zero and a template nothing can reach is not content.
# The commander's bc_dust_and_thirst predates that measurement and is left alone.
#
# TWO OF THE THREE READ A PURSUIT RATHER THAN A FIGHT, which is what the ground
# is for on this side: the commander's band cases ask what the terrain does to an
# army, and a knight can only report what it did to the fifty men he can see.
#
# NONE OF THE THREE IS A SUBSET of a shipped gate: rough + prowess >= 10 shares
# the prowess floor with holds_the_breach (which wants losing) and
# stands_in_the_snow (which wants winter); forest + winning shares the side row
# with unhorses_a_lord (prowess >= 12) and takes_a_captive (prowess >= 8 and
# outnumbering); jungle has no row any other knight case has.
# ===========================================================================

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# On the broken ground. The commonest ground in the game, read from inside the
# ranks - 58 of 137 in the census, more than forest and winter together.
#
# THE PROWESS ROW IS THE SECOND CONDITION rough needs, and it is also the case:
# nothing mounted works here, so what is left is a man who is worth something on
# his feet. bc_the_broken_ground_holds is the commander's rough case and pairs
# the band with losing instead, so the two do not shadow each other.
#
# `entrenched` AND NO RUNG. bc_shieldwall lands on the side either way, which is
# the right consequence - men who have stopped trying to manoeuvre on this
# ground are harder to move and slower to do anything - but a rung would say one
# knight's footing decided the battle.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_on_the_broken_ground = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		prowess >= 10
		bc_field_is_rough_trigger = yes
	}

	chance = {
		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		# A man who was never going to give ground is more use where there is
		# no good ground to give.
		modifier = {
			add = 40
			has_trait = stubborn
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_trait = craven
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_on_the_broken_ground"

		add_prestige = 50

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes
			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = minor KIND = entrenched SWING = up }

			send_interface_toast = {
				type = event_toast_effect_good
				title = bc_knight_on_the_broken_ground_toast
				desc = bc_knight_on_the_broken_ground_toast_desc
				left_icon = scope:bc_knight
			}
		}
	}
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The pursuit dies in the wood. Forest, and the side is winning.
#
# THE MOD'S SECOND `ordered`/`down` and the reason the pair is worth having at
# all: a chase that goes into trees stops being a chase and becomes fifty men
# walking about separately. The verb table maps the pair to nothing, so this
# takes bc_pursuit away from nobody - it simply declines to hand it out, which
# is exactly what the moment is.
#
# THE WINNING ROW IS THE CASE. Forest while losing is cover and is
# bc_the_woods_hide_us's story on the commander side; forest while winning is
# the trees taking back what the line just won.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
bc_knight_the_wood_takes_them = {
	type = knight

	is_valid = {
		is_alive = yes
		bc_chronicle_ready_trigger = yes
		bc_field_is_forest_trigger = yes
		bc_side_is_winning_trigger = yes
	}

	chance = {
		base = 80
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_low
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 2
			has_game_rule = bc_frequency_high
		}
		# A man in a hurry goes further into it before he notices.
		modifier = {
			add = 40
			has_trait = impatient
		}
		modifier = {
			factor = 0.5
			has_trait = patient
		}
	}

	effect = {
		save_scope_as = bc_knight
		bc_mark_chronicle_effect = yes
		debug_log = "BC: fired bc_knight_the_wood_takes_them"

		add_stress = 5

		scope:combat_side.side_commander ?= {
			bc_battle_open_effect = yes
			bc_record_moment_effect = { TIER = minor KIND = ordered SWING = down }
